## Financial Administration Act {#financial-administration-act .Title-of-Act}

[R.S.C.]{.abbr title="Revised Statutes of Canada"}, 1985, c. F-11
:::

An Act to provide for the financial administration of the Government of
Canada, the establishment and maintenance of the accounts of Canada and
the control of Crown corporations
::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-227967 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Act may be cited as
the [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. F-10, s. 1
:::

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-227972 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** In this Act,

[[appropriate Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[appropriate Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a department named in Schedule I,
      the Minister presiding over the department,

    - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a division or branch of the
      federal public administration set out in column I of Schedule I.1,
      the Minister set out in column II of that Schedule,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a commission under the [Inquiries
      Act](/eng/acts/I-11), the Minister designated by order of the
      Governor in Council as the appropriate Minister,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} with respect to the Senate and the office of the
      Senate Ethics Officer, the Speaker of the Senate, with respect to
      the House of Commons, the Board of Internal Economy, with respect
      to the office of the Conflict of Interest and Ethics Commissioner,
      the Speaker of the House of Commons, and with respect to the
      Library of Parliament, the Parliamentary Protective Service and
      the office of the Parliamentary Budget Officer, the Speakers of
      the Senate and the House of Commons,

    - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a departmental corporation, the
      Minister designated by order of the Governor in Council as the
      appropriate Minister, and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a Crown corporation, the
      appropriate Minister as defined in subsection 83(1); ([ministre
      compétent]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[appropriation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[appropriation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any authority of
    Parliament to pay money out of the Consolidated Revenue
    Fund; ([crédit]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Auditor General of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Auditor General of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the officer
    appointed pursuant to subsection 3(1) of the [Auditor General
    Act](/eng/acts/A-17); ([vérificateur général]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[authorized agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[authorized agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any person authorized
    by the Minister to accept subscriptions for or make sales of
    securities; ([agent agréé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Consolidated Revenue Fund]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Consolidated Revenue Fund]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
    aggregate of all public moneys that are on deposit at the credit of
    the Receiver General; ([Trésor]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Crown corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Crown corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned
    by subsection 83(1); ([société d'État]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[department]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[department]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any of the departments named in Schedule I,

    - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} any of the divisions or branches of the federal
      public administration set out in column I of Schedule I.1,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a commission under the [Inquiries
      Act](/eng/acts/I-11) that is designated by order of the Governor
      in Council as a department for the purposes of this Act,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the staffs of the Senate, House of Commons,
      Library of Parliament, office of the Senate Ethics Officer, office
      of the Conflict of Interest and Ethics Commissioner, Parliamentary
      Protective Service and office of the Parliamentary Budget Officer,
      and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any departmental
      corporation; ([ministère]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[departmental corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[departmental corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a corporation
    named in Schedule II; ([établissement public]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[fiscal agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fiscal agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a fiscal agent appointed
    under Part IV and includes the Bank of Canada; ([agent
    financier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[fiscal year]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fiscal year]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the period beginning on
    April 1 in one year and ending on March 31 in the next
    year; ([exercice]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of
    Finance; ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[money]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[money]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes negotiable
    instruments; ([fonds]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[negotiable instrument]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[negotiable instrument]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes any cheque,
    draft, traveller's cheque, bill of exchange, postal note, money
    order, postal remittance and any other similar instrument; ([effet
    de commerce]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[non-certificated security]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[non-certificated security]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a
    security for which no certificate is issued and a certificated
    security held within a security clearing and settlement system in
    the custody of a custodian or nominee; ([valeur sans
    certificat]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[parent Crown corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[parent Crown corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning
    assigned by subsection 83(1); ([société d'État
    mère]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[public money]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[public money]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means all money belonging to
    Canada received or collected by the Receiver General or any other
    public officer in his official capacity or any person authorized to
    receive or collect such money, and includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} duties and revenues of Canada,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} money borrowed by Canada or received through the
      issue or sale of securities,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} money received or collected for or on behalf of
      Canada, and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all money that is paid to or received or
      collected by a public officer under or pursuant to any Act, trust,
      treaty, undertaking or contract, and is to be disbursed for a
      purpose specified in or pursuant to that Act, trust, treaty,
      undertaking or contract; ([fonds publics]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[public officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[public officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a minister of the
    Crown and any person employed in the federal public
    administration; ([fonctionnaire public]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[public property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[public property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means all property, other
    than money, belonging to Her Majesty in right of Canada; ([biens
    publics]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[registrar]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[registrar]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a registrar appointed under
    Part IV and includes the Bank of Canada; ([agent
    comptable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[securities]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[securities]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means securities of Canada in
    certificated form or non-certificated securities of Canada, and
    includes bonds, notes, deposit certificates, non-interest bearing
    certificates, debentures, treasury bills, treasury notes and any
    other security representing part of the public debt of
    Canada; ([valeurs]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"} ou
    [titres]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[security certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[security certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a tangible
    certificate issued by or on behalf of Her Majesty representing part
    of the public debt of Canada; ([certificat de
    valeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[treasury bill]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[treasury bill]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a bill in certificated
    form, or a non-certificated security, issued by or on behalf of Her
    Majesty for the payment of a principal sum specified in the bill to
    a named recipient or to a bearer at a date not later than twelve
    months after the date of issue of the bill; ([bon du
    Trésor]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[treasury note]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[treasury note]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a note in certificated
    form, or a non-certificated security, issued by or on behalf of Her
    Majesty for the payment of a principal sum specified in the note to
    a named recipient or to a bearer at a date not later than twelve
    months after the date of issue of the note. ([billet du
    Trésor]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 2
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (4th Supp.), s. 25

:::

## [Alteration of Schedules]{.HTitleText1} {#h-228015 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Addition to Schedule I.1, II or III

- **[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, by order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} add to Schedule I.1 in column I thereof the name of
    any division or branch of the federal public administration and in
    column II thereof opposite that name a reference to the appropriate
    Minister;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} add to Schedule II the name of any corporation
    established by an Act of Parliament that performs administrative,
    research, supervisory, advisory or regulatory functions of a
    governmental nature; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} add to Part I or II of Schedule III the name of any
    parent Crown corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alteration of Schedule I.1

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order, amend
  Schedule I.1 by striking out the reference in column II thereof
  opposite the name of a division or branch of the federal public
  administration in column I thereof and by substituting therefor
  another reference in column II thereof opposite that name.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order, delete from
  Schedule I.1 the name of any division or branch of the federal public
  administration that has been changed and shall thereupon add the new
  name of the division or branch to that Schedule.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order, delete from
  Schedule I.1 the name of any division or branch of the federal public
  administration that has ceased to exist, become part of another
  department or otherwise ceased to be a separate division or branch of
  the federal public administration and the reference to the appropriate
  Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alteration of Schedule II or III

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} delete from Schedule II the name of any corporation
    that has been changed and shall, by the same order, add the new name
    of the corporation to that Schedule; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} delete from Part I or II of Schedule III the name
    of any parent Crown corporation that has been changed and shall, by
    the same order, add the new name of the corporation to that Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order, delete from
  Part I or II of Schedule III the name of any parent Crown corporation
  that should appear in the other Part and shall, by the same order, add
  the name of that corporation to the other Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The name of a parent Crown corporation shall not be
  added to Schedule III, if the Governor in Council is satisfied that
  the corporation meets the criteria described in paragraph (1)(a.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The name of a parent Crown corporation shall not be
  added to Part II of Schedule III, unless the Governor in Council is
  satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the corporation

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} operates in a competitive environment,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is not ordinarily dependent on appropriations
      for operating purposes, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} ordinarily earns a return on equity; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is a reasonable expectation that the
    corporation will pay dividends.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deletion from Schedule II or III

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} delete from Schedule II the name of any corporation
    that has been dissolved or otherwise has ceased to be a corporation
    described in paragraph (1)(a.1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} delete from Part I or II of Schedule III the name
    of any corporation that has been dissolved or otherwise has ceased
    to be a parent Crown corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Addition to Schedule IV or V

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order, add to
  Schedule IV or V the name of any portion of the federal public
  administration

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to which Part I of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2) does not apply; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of which a minister of the Crown, the
    Treasury Board or the Governor in Council is authorized to establish
    or approve terms and conditions of employment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfers between Schedules IV and V

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order, delete the
  name of any portion of the federal public administration named in
  Schedule IV or V, in which case the Governor in Council must add the
  name of that portion to the other one of those two schedules, but the
  Governor in Council need not do so if that portion

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} no longer has any employees; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is a corporation that has been excluded from the
    operation of Part I of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of [Canada Labour
  Code](/eng/acts/L-2)

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The exclusion of a corporation from the operation of
  Part I of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) ceases to have
  effect if the corporation's name is deleted from Schedule IV or V
  without a corresponding addition to the other one of those two
  schedules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Schedule VI

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} add to Part I of Schedule VI the name of any
    department named in Schedule I;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} add to Part II or III of Schedule VI the name of
    any department and a reference to the accounting officer for the
    department;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} amend Part II or III of Schedule VI by replacing a
    reference to the accounting officer for a department with a new
    reference;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} move from Part II to Part III of Schedule VI, or
    from Part III to Part II of that Schedule, the name of a department
    and the reference to its accounting officer;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} amend Part I, II or III of Schedule VI by replacing
    the former name of a department with the new name; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} delete the name of a department and the reference
    to its accounting officer from Part I, II or III of Schedule VI,
    where the department has ceased to exist or become part of another
    department.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 3
- 1991, c. 24, s. 1
- 1992, c. 1, s. 70
- 1999, c. 31, s. 99
- 2003, c. 22, ss. 3, 224(E)
- 2006, c. 9, s. 257
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-3-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tabling order

- **[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  order made pursuant to subsection 3(3) that deletes the name of a
  corporation from Part I of Schedule III and adds it to Part II of that
  Schedule shall be laid before each House of Parliament within the
  first fifteen days on which that House is sitting after the order is
  made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to committee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order laid before a House of Parliament pursuant
  to subsection (1) stands referred to such committee of that House as
  may be designated or established by that House for the purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delay

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order laid before a House of Parliament pursuant
  to subsection (1) shall come into force on the thirty-first sitting
  day after the order has been laid before both Houses of Parliament or
  on such later day as is specified in the order.

- Definition of [[sitting day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[sitting day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  means a day on which either House of Parliament is sitting.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 4
- 1999, c. 31, s. 100(F)
:::

## [PART I]{.HLabel1}[Organization]{.HTitleText1} {#h-228078 .Part}

### [Treasury Board]{.HTitleText2} {#h-228079 .Subheading}

#### [Establishment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-228080 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Treasury Board

- **[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There is
  hereby established a committee of the Queen's Privy Council for Canada
  called the Treasury Board over which the President of the Treasury
  Board appointed by Commission under the Great Seal shall preside.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of committee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board shall, in addition to the
  President of the Treasury Board, consist of the Minister and four
  other members of the Queen's Privy Council for Canada to be nominated
  from time to time by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternate members

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may nominate such additional
  members of the Queen's Privy Council for Canada as he sees fit to be
  alternates to serve in the place of members of the Treasury Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules and procedures

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act and any directions of the
  Governor in Council, the Treasury Board may determine its own rules
  and procedures.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. F-10, s. 3
:::

#### [Public Officers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-228092 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of President

- **[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  President of the Treasury Board holds office during pleasure and
  presides over meetings of the Treasury Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Secretary of the Treasury Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may appoint an officer called
  the Secretary of the Treasury Board to hold office during pleasure,
  which officer ranks as and has the powers of a deputy head of a
  department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief Human Resources Officer

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may appoint an officer
  called the Chief Human Resources Officer to hold office during
  pleasure, who ranks as and has the powers of a deputy head of a
  department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Comptroller General of Canada

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may appoint an officer called
  the Comptroller General of Canada to hold office during pleasure,
  which officer ranks as and has the powers of a deputy head of a
  department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief Information Officer of Canada

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may appoint an officer
  called the Chief Information Officer of Canada to hold office during
  pleasure, who ranks as and has the powers of a deputy head of a
  department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board may delegate to the President of
  the Treasury Board, to the Secretary of the Treasury Board, to the
  Comptroller General of Canada or to the deputy head or chief executive
  officer of any portion of the federal public administration any of the
  powers or functions it is authorized to exercise under any Act of
  Parliament or by any order made by the Governor in Council. It may
  make the delegation subject to any terms and conditions that it
  considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation to Chief Human Resources
  Officer

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board may, subject to any terms and
  conditions that it considers appropriate, delegate to the Chief Human
  Resources Officer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any of the powers or functions in relation to human
    resources management, official languages, employment equity, and
    values and ethics that it is authorized to exercise under any Act of
    Parliament or by any order made by the Governor in Council; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any of the powers or functions in relation to
    employment that it is authorized to exercise under the [Public
    Service Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation to Chief Information Officer
  of Canada

  [(4.11)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board may, subject to any terms and
  conditions that it considers appropriate, delegate to the Chief
  Information Officer of Canada any of the powers or functions that it
  is authorized to exercise under any Act of Parliament or by any order
  made by the Governor in Council, including powers or functions in
  relation to information technology.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}President of Treasury Board to
  coordinate activities

  [(4.2)]{.lawlabel} The President of the Treasury Board is responsible
  and accountable for the coordination of the activities of the
  Secretary of the Treasury Board, the Chief Human Resources Officer,
  the Comptroller General of Canada and the Chief Information Officer of
  Canada and may, subject to any terms and conditions that the President
  of the Treasury Board considers appropriate, delegate that
  responsibility to the Secretary of the Treasury Board or to any person
  under the President of the Treasury Board's jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (4), (4.1) and (4.11) do not apply in
  respect of the Treasury Board's power to delegate under those
  subsections or to its power to make regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sub-delegation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Any person to whom powers or functions are delegated
  under subsection (4), (4.1) or (4.11) may, subject to and in
  accordance with the delegation, sub-delegate any of those powers or
  functions to any person under their jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officers and employees

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The other officers and employees that are necessary
  for the proper conduct of the business of the Treasury Board are to be
  appointed in the manner authorized by the [Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 6
- 1991, c. 24, s. 50(F)
- 2003, c. 22, s. 5
- 2005, c. 15, s. 4
- 2010, c. 12, s. 1675
- 2018, c. 12, s. 199
:::

:::

#### [Responsibilities and Powers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-228122 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of Treasury Board

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Treasury Board may act for the Queen's Privy Council for Canada on all
  matters relating to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} general administrative policy in the federal public
    administration;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the organization of the federal public
    administration or any portion thereof, and the determination and
    control of establishments therein;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} financial management, including estimates,
    expenditures, financial commitments, accounts, fees or charges for
    the provision of services or the use of facilities, rentals,
    licences, leases, revenues from the disposition of property, and
    procedures by which departments manage, record and account for
    revenues received or receivable from any source whatever;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the review of annual and longer term expenditure
    plans and programs of departments, and the determination of
    priorities with respect thereto;

  - [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} the management and development by departments of
    lands, other than Canada Lands as defined in subsection 24(1) of the
    [Canada Lands Surveys Act](/eng/acts/L-6);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} human resources management in the federal public
    administration, including the determination of the terms and
    conditions of employment of persons employed in it;

  - [(e.1)]{.lawlabel} the terms and conditions of employment of persons
    appointed by the Governor in Council that have not been established
    under this or any other Act of Parliament or order in council or by
    any other means; and

  - [(e.2)]{.lawlabel} internal audit in the federal public
    administration;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} such other matters as may be referred to it by the
    Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority under other Acts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board may exercise the powers, other
  than powers of appointment, of the Governor in Council under

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the [Public Service Superannuation
    Act](/eng/acts/P-36);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the [Canadian Forces Superannuation
    Act](/eng/acts/C-17);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the [Defence Services Pension Continuation
    Act](/eng/acts/D-1.3), chapter D-3 of the Revised Statutes of
    Canada, 1970;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} Parts I and II of the [Royal Canadian Mounted
    Police Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/R-11);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police Pension
    Continuation Act](/eng/acts/R-10.6), chapter R-10 of the Revised
    Statutes of Canada, 1970; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} such of the provisions of any other Act respecting
    any matter in relation to which the Treasury Board may act for the
    Queen's Privy Council for Canada pursuant to subsection (1) as may
    be specified by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order, authorize the
  Treasury Board to exercise all or any of the powers of the Governor in
  Council under section 41 or subsection 122(1) or (6) and specify the
  circumstances in which those powers may be exercised.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Services to departments, Crown
  corporations and other entities

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board may, in carrying out its
  responsibilities under subsection (1), provide services to departments
  and Crown corporations. With the authorization of the Governor in
  Council, it may also provide these services to a provincial
  government, a municipality in Canada, a provincial or municipal public
  body or any other public body performing a function of government in
  Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Access to Information
  Act](/eng/acts/A-1)

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, for the purposes of the
  [Access to Information Act](/eng/acts/A-1), the records of an entity
  to which the Treasury Board provides services under subsection (4)
  that are, on behalf of that entity, contained in or carried on the
  Treasury Board's information technology systems are not under the
  control of the Treasury Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21)

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, for the purposes of the
  [Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21), personal information that is collected
  by an entity to which the Treasury Board provides services under
  subsection (4) and that is, on behalf of that entity, contained in or
  carried on the Treasury Board's information technology systems is not
  under the control of the Treasury Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 7
- 1991, c. 24, ss. 2, 49(E)
- 1998, c. 14, s. 103(F)
- 2003, c. 22, ss. 6, 224(E)
- 2006, c. 9, s. 258
- [2022, c. 10, s. 255](#1357314-1366559){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357314-1366559.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7-20061212.html "Link to previous version of section 7"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Group insurance and benefit programs

- **[[7.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Treasury Board may establish or modify any group insurance or other
  benefit programs for employees of the federal public administration
  and any other persons or classes of persons it may designate to be
  members of those programs, may take any measure necessary for that
  purpose, including contracting for services, may set any terms and
  conditions in respect of those programs, including those relating to
  premiums, contributions, benefits, management, control and
  expenditures and may audit and make payments in respect of those
  programs, including payments relating to premiums, contributions,
  benefits and other expenditures.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remainder of Act does not apply

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This Act, other than this section, does not apply to
  any contributions or other payments made or premiums paid by the
  Treasury Board or the members in respect of any program established or
  modified pursuant to subsection (1) or any benefits received by the
  members of such a program.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 18, s. 3
- 2003, c. 22, s. 7(E)
- 2005, c. 30, ss. 132, 134(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7.1-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 7.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation by letters patent

- **[[7.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  the recommendation of the National Joint Council of the Public
  Service, the President of the Treasury Board may issue letters patent
  of incorporation that take effect on the date stated in them for a
  corporation without share capital that is charged with the
  administration of any group insurance or benefit program described in
  subsection 7.1(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of letters patent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The letters patent must set out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the corporation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the programs described in subsection 7.1(1) in
    respect of which the corporation is charged with the administration;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the objects and powers of the corporation necessary
    for it to fulfil its purpose under subsection (1);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the appointment and the operations of the
    corporation's board of directors;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the corporation's reporting obligations;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the corporation's obligations with respect to
    audits of its accounts and financial transactions by an independent
    auditor;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the code of conduct for the corporation's directors
    and officers; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any other provision that is necessary to fulfil the
    corporation's purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supplementary letters patent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The President of the Treasury Board may, on the
  recommendation of the National Joint Council of the Public Service,
  after consulting with the board of directors, issue supplementary
  letters patent amending the corporation's letters patent and the
  supplementary letters patent take effect on the date stated in them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The corporation's letters patent and supplementary
  letters patent are not regulations within the meaning of the
  [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22). However, they must be
  published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Capacity of a natural person

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The corporation has, subject to its letters patent
  and this Act, the capacity of a natural person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status of corporation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The corporation is neither a Crown corporation nor an
  agent of Her Majesty.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 30, s. 132
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board of directors

**[[7.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.3 .sectionLabel}** The corporation's board
of directors consists of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one director appointed by the President of the
  Treasury Board, on the recommendation of the National Joint Council of
  the Public Service, who is the chairperson;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} one director appointed by the President of the
  Treasury Board, on the recommendation of the National Joint Council of
  the Public Service, who, in the opinion of the President, represents
  the pensioners;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} four directors appointed by the President of the
  Treasury Board; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} four directors appointed by that portion of the
  National Joint Council of the Public Service that represents the
  employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 30, s. 132
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[7.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.4 .sectionLabel}** The Treasury Board may
make regulations respecting the governance of the corporation, including
regulations that adapt any provisions of the [Canada Business
Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-44) and the [Canada Not-for-profit
Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-7.75) and any regulations made under those
Acts for the purpose of applying those provisions as adapted to the
corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 30, s. 132
- 2009, c. 23, ss. 327, 353
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7.4-20111017.html "Link to previous version of section 7.4"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subject to directions of Governor in
Council

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** The Treasury Board in the
exercise of its powers under this Act or any other Act of Parliament is
subject to any direction given to it by the Governor in Council, and the
Governor in Council may, by order, amend or revoke any action of the
Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. F-10, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form of accounts of Canada

- **[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Treasury Board may prescribe from time to time the manner and form in
  which the accounts of Canada and the accounts of the several
  departments shall be kept, and may direct any person receiving,
  managing or disbursing public money to keep any books, records or
  accounts that the Board considers necessary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Land management and development records
  and plans

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} require departments to maintain records and prepare
    plans with respect to the management and development of lands under
    paragraph 7(1)(d.1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribe the manner and form in which the records
    and plans are to be maintained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Production of documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board may require from any public
  officer or any agent of Her Majesty any account, return, statement,
  document, report or information that the Board considers necessary for
  the due performance of its duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board may require any public officer or
  agent of Her Majesty to provide a department with such information
  from any account, return, record, statement, document or report as may
  be required

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to locate any person in order to collect a debt due
    to Her Majesty in right of Canada by that person; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to set off a debt due to Her Majesty in right of
    Canada or a province against any sum of money that may be due or
    payable by Her Majesty in right of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 9
- 1991, c. 24, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** Subject to any other Act
of Parliament, the Treasury Board may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of ensuring effective coordination of
  administrative functions and services among and within departments;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the establishment of general administrative
  standards of performance and respecting the assessment of the
  performance of portions of the federal public administration in the
  light of such standards;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the collection, management and
  administration of, and the accounting for, public money;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the keeping of records of public property;

- [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} respecting the severance pay or other amounts
  payable to an employee or former employee whose employment is
  terminated pursuant to paragraph 11(2)(g.1), and any terms and
  conditions subject to which and the manner in which those amounts are
  to be paid;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of any provision of this Act that
  contemplates regulations of the Treasury Board; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} for any other purpose necessary for the efficient
  administration of the federal public administration.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 10
- 1991, c. 24, s. 50(F)
- 1996, c. 18, s. 4
- 2003, c. 22, s. 224(E)
:::

Version](section-10-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 10"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Human Resources Management]{.HTitleText3} {#h-228219 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in this section and sections 11.1 to 13.

  [[core public administration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[core public administration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
      departments named in Schedule I and the other portions of the
      federal public administration named in Schedule
      IV. ([administration publique centrale]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[deputy head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[deputy head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in relation to a department named in Schedule
        I, its deputy minister;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in relation to any portion of the federal
        public administration named in Schedule IV, its chief executive
        officer or, if there is no chief executive officer, its
        statutory deputy head or, if there is neither, the person who
        occupies the position designated under subsection (2) in respect
        of that portion;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in relation to a separate agency, its chief
        executive officer or, if there is no chief executive officer,
        its statutory deputy head or, if there is neither, the person
        who occupies the position designated under subsection (2) in
        respect of that separate agency; and

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in relation to any portion of the federal
        public administration designated for the purposes of
        paragraph (d) of the definition [[public
        service]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, its chief executive officer or,
        if there is no chief executive officer, the person who occupies
        the position designated under subsection (2) in respect of that
        portion. ([administrateur général]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[public service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[public service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the several positions
      in or under

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the departments named in Schedule I;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the other portions of the federal public
        administration named in Schedule IV;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the separate agencies named in Schedule V; and

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other portion of the federal public
        administration that may be designated by the Governor in Council
        for the purpose of this paragraph. ([fonction
        publique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[separate agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[separate agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a portion of the
      federal public administration named in Schedule V. ([organisme
      distinct]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[statutory deputy head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[statutory deputy head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any officer
      who, by any Act of Parliament, is or is deemed to be a deputy head
      or who has, or is deemed to have, the rank of a deputy
      head. ([administrateur général au titre de la
      loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation of certain deputy heads

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may designate any position to
  be the position of deputy head in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any portion of the federal public administration
    named in Schedule IV or V for which there is no chief executive
    officer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} each portion of the federal public administration
    designated for the purpose of paragraph (d) of the definition
    [[public service]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection (1) for which
    there is no chief executive officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 11
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 22
- 1991, c. 24, s. 50(F)
- 1992, c. 54, s. 81
- 1995, c. 44, s. 51
- 1996, c. 18, s. 5
- 1999, c. 31, s. 101(F)
- 2003, c. 22, ss. 8, 264
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 11"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of the Treasury Board

- **[[11.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  the exercise of its human resources management responsibilities under
  paragraph 7(1)(e), the Treasury Board may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine the human resources requirements of the
    public service and provide for the allocation and effective
    utilization of human resources in the public service;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide for the classification of positions and
    persons employed in the public service;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} determine and regulate the pay to which persons
    employed in the public service are entitled for services rendered,
    the hours of work and leave of those persons and any related
    matters;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} determine and regulate the payments that may be
    made to persons employed in the public service by way of
    reimbursement for travel or other expenses and by way of allowances
    in respect of expenses and conditions arising out of their
    employment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} subject to the [Employment Equity
    Act](/eng/acts/E-5.401), establish policies and programs with
    respect to the implementation of employment equity in the public
    service;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} establish policies or issue directives respecting
    the exercise of the powers granted by this Act to deputy heads in
    the core public administration and the reporting by those deputy
    heads in respect of the exercise of those powers;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} establish policies or issue directives respecting

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which deputy heads in the core
      public administration may deal with grievances under the [Federal
      Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) to which
      they are a party, and the manner in which they may deal with them
      if the grievances are referred to adjudication under subsection
      209(1) or 238.25(1) of that Act, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the reporting by those deputy heads in respect
      of those grievances;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} establish policies or issue directives respecting
    the disclosure by persons employed in the public service of
    information concerning wrongdoing in the public service and the
    protection from reprisal of persons who disclose such information in
    accordance with those policies or directives;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} establish policies or issue directives respecting
    the prevention of harassment in the workplace and the resolution of
    disputes relating to such harassment; and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} provide for any other matters, including terms and
    conditions of employment not otherwise specifically provided for in
    this section, that it considers necessary for effective human
    resources management in the public service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The powers of the Treasury Board in relation to any
  of the matters specified in subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} do not extend to any matter that is expressly
    determined, fixed, provided for, regulated or established by any Act
    otherwise than by the conferring of powers in relation to those
    matters on any authority or person specified in that Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} do not include or extend to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any power specifically conferred on the Public
      Service Commission under the [Public Service Employment
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any process of human resources selection
      required to be used under the [Public Service Employment
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01) or authorized to be used by the Public
      Service Commission under that Act, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any power specifically conferred on the
      Commissioner of the Royal Canadian Mounted Police under paragraph
      20.2(1)(l) of the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police
      Act](/eng/acts/R-10).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 8
- 2013, c. 18, s. 46
- 2017, c. 9, s. 44
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.1-20141128.html "Link to previous version of section 11.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation by Governor in Council

- **[[11.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may delegate
  to the minister of the Crown responsible for a separate agency, or to
  its deputy head, any of the powers or functions of the Governor in
  Council or the Treasury Board in relation to human resources
  management in that separate agency, subject to any terms and
  conditions that the Governor in Council directs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sub-delegation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any person to whom powers or functions are delegated
  under subsection (1) may, subject to and in accordance with the
  delegation, sub-delegate any of those powers or functions to any
  person under their jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of deputy heads in core public
administration

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to paragraphs 11.1(1)(f) and
  (g), every deputy head in the core public administration may, with
  respect to the portion for which he or she is deputy head,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine the learning, training and development
    requirements of persons employed in the public service and fix the
    terms on which the learning, training and development may be carried
    out;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide for the awards that may be made to persons
    employed in the public service for outstanding performance of their
    duties, for other meritorious achievement in relation to their
    duties or for inventions or practical suggestions for improvements;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} establish standards of discipline and set
    penalties, including termination of employment, suspension, demotion
    to a position at a lower maximum rate of pay and financial
    penalties;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} provide for the termination of employment, or the
    demotion to a position at a lower maximum rate of pay, of persons
    employed in the public service whose performance, in the opinion of
    the deputy head, is unsatisfactory;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} provide for the termination of employment, or the
    demotion to a position at a lower maximum rate of pay, of persons
    employed in the public service for reasons other than breaches of
    discipline or misconduct; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} provide for the termination of employment of
    persons to whom an offer of employment is made as the result of the
    transfer of any work, undertaking or business from the core public
    administration to any body or corporation that is not part of the
    core public administration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of other deputy heads

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any terms and conditions that the Governor
  in Council may direct, every deputy head of a separate agency, and
  every deputy head designated under paragraph 11(2)(b), may, with
  respect to the portion of the federal public administration for which
  he or she is deputy head,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine the learning, training and development
    requirements of persons employed in the public service and fixing
    the terms on which the learning, training and development may be
    carried out;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide for the awards that may be made to persons
    employed in the public service for outstanding performance of their
    duties, for other meritorious achievement in relation to their
    duties or for inventions or practical suggestions for improvements;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} establish standards of discipline and set
    penalties, including termination of employment, suspension, demotion
    to a position at a lower maximum rate of pay and financial
    penalties; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} provide for the termination of employment, or the
    demotion to a position at a lower maximum rate of pay, of persons
    employed in the public service for reasons other than breaches of
    discipline or misconduct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For cause

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Disciplinary action against, or the termination of
  employment or the demotion of, any person under paragraph (1)(c), (d)
  or (e) or (2)(c) or (d) may only be for cause.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 12
- 1995, c. 17, s. 7
- 1996, c. 18, s. 6
- 2003, c. 22, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

**[[12.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.1 .sectionLabel}** Section 11.1 and
subsection 12(2) apply subject to the provisions of any Act of
Parliament, or any regulation, order or other instrument made under the
authority of an Act of Parliament, respecting the powers or functions of
a separate agency.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation by deputy head

- **[[12.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  deputy head may delegate to any person any of the deputy head's powers
  or functions in relation to human resources management, subject to any
  terms and conditions that he or she directs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sub-delegation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any person to whom powers or functions are delegated
  under subsection (1) may, subject to and in accordance with the
  delegation, sub-delegate any of those powers or functions to any other
  person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}National Joint Council agreements

- **[[12.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other Act of Parliament,
  if the employment of an employee is terminated under paragraph
  12(1)(f), agreements of the National Joint Council, other than
  agreements of the National Joint Council that are related to work
  force adjustment, cease to apply to the employee immediately before
  the termination of employment, unless the termination of employment
  was the result of the transfer of any work, undertaking or business
  from the core public administration to any body or corporation that is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a separate agency; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} another portion of the federal public
    administration designated by the Governor in Council for the purpose
    of paragraph (d) of the definition [[public
    service]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 11(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accrued benefits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of Canada, as represented by the
  Treasury Board, continues to be responsible for any obligation of Her
  Majesty in respect of benefits arising out of agreements of the
  National Joint Council that had accrued to employees of a body or
  corporation immediately before the date of the transfer referred to in
  subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 8
:::

**[[12.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.4 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2012, c.
19, s. 218\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.4-20051231.html "Link to previous version of section 12.4"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right or power of Governor in Council not
affected

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), nothing
  in this Act or any other Act of Parliament is to be construed as
  limiting or affecting the right or power of the Governor in Council to
  suspend or dismiss, on the basis of a security assessment, any person
  employed in the public service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person has made a complaint with respect to a
  security assessment to the National Security and Intelligence Review
  Agency, that person may not be dismissed by the Governor in Council
  until after the completion of the investigation in relation to that
  complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to be conclusive proof

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), any order made by
  the Governor in Council is conclusive proof of the matters stated
  therein in relation to the suspension or dismissal of any person in
  the interest of the safety or security of Canada or any state allied
  or associated with Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 13
- 1991, c. 24, s. 50(F)
- 2003, c. 22, s. 9
- [2019, c. 13, s. 26](#1158759-1193089){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1158759-1193089.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 13"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Department of Finance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-228322 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Department established

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** There is hereby
established a department of the Government of Canada called the
Department of Finance over which the Minister of Finance appointed by
commission under the Great Seal shall preside.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. F-10, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** The Minister holds office
during pleasure and has the management and direction of the Department,
the management of the Consolidated Revenue Fund and the supervision,
control and direction of all matters relating to the financial affairs
of Canada not by law assigned to the Treasury Board or to any other
minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. F-10, s. 9
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advisory and other committees

- **[[15.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may establish advisory
  and other committees and provide for their membership, duties,
  functions and operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Members of a committee may be paid for their services
  the remuneration and expenses that the Governor in Council may
  determine.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 28, s. 153
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deputy head

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may appoint an officer called the Deputy Minister of Finance to hold
office during pleasure and to be the deputy head of the Department.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. F-10, s. 10
:::

## [PART I.1]{.HLabel1}[Internal Audit and Accounting Officers]{.HTitleText1} {#h-228342 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Audit capacity

**[[16.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.1 .sectionLabel}** The deputy head or
chief executive officer of a department is responsible for ensuring an
internal audit capacity appropriate to the needs of the department.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2006, c. 9, s. 259
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Audit committees

**[[16.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.2 .sectionLabel}** Subject to and except
as otherwise provided in any directives issued by the Treasury Board
under paragraph 7(1)(e.2), the deputy head or chief executive officer of
a department shall establish an audit committee for the department.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2006, c. 9, s. 259
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

- **[[16.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who does not occupy a
  position in the federal public administration but who meets the
  qualifications established by directive of the Treasury Board may be
  appointed to an audit committee by the Treasury Board on the
  recommendation of the President of the Treasury Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of office

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A member of an audit committee so appointed holds
  office during pleasure for a term not exceeding four years, which may
  be renewed for a second term.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A member of an audit committee so appointed shall be
  paid the remuneration and expenses fixed by the Treasury Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2006, c. 9, s. 259
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[accounting
officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[16.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.3 .sectionLabel}** In sections 16.4 and
16.5, [[accounting officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a department named in Part I of
  Schedule VI, means its deputy minister; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a department named in Part II or III
  of Schedule VI, means the person occupying the position set out
  opposite that name.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2006, c. 9, s. 259
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accountability of accounting officers
within framework of ministerial accountability

- **[[16.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within the framework of the
  appropriate minister's responsibilities and his or her accountability
  to Parliament, and subject to the appropriate minister's management
  and direction of his or her department, the accounting officer of a
  department named in Part I of Schedule VI is accountable before the
  appropriate committees of the Senate and the House of Commons for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the measures taken to organize the resources of the
    department to deliver departmental programs in compliance with
    government policies and procedures;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the measures taken to maintain effective systems of
    internal control in the department;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the signing of the accounts that are required to be
    kept for the preparation of the Public Accounts pursuant to section
    64; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the performance of other specific duties assigned
    to him or her by or under this or any other Act in relation to the
    administration of the department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accountability of accounting officers
  within framework of ministerial accountability

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Within the framework of the appropriate minister's
  responsibilities under the Act or order constituting the department
  and his or her accountability to Parliament, the accounting officer of
  a department named in Part II or III of Schedule VI is accountable
  before the appropriate committees of the Senate and the House of
  Commons for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the measures taken to organize the resources of the
    department to deliver departmental programs in compliance with
    government policies and procedures;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the measures taken to maintain effective systems of
    internal control in the department;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the signing of the accounts that are required to be
    kept for the preparation of the Public Accounts pursuant to section
    64; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the performance of other specific duties assigned
    to him or her by or under this or any other Act in relation to the
    administration of the department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appearance before committee

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The obligation of an accounting officer under this
  section is to appear before the appropriate committee of the Senate or
  the House of Commons and answer questions put to him or her by members
  of the committee in respect of the carrying out of the
  responsibilities and the performance of the duties referred to in
  subsection (1) or (2), as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2006, c. 9, s. 259
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written guidance from Secretary

- **[[16.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the appropriate minister and
  the accounting officer for a department named in Part I or II of
  Schedule VI are unable to agree on the interpretation or application
  of a policy, directive or standard issued by the Treasury Board, the
  accounting officer shall seek guidance in writing on the matter from
  the Secretary of the Treasury Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral to Treasury Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where guidance is provided under subsection (1) and
  the matter remains unresolved, the appropriate minister shall refer
  the matter to the Treasury Board for a decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to Auditor General

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A decision by the Treasury Board shall be in writing
  and a copy shall be provided to the Auditor General of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cabinet confidence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The copy of a decision provided to the Auditor
  General of Canada is a confidence of the Queen's Privy Council for
  Canada for the purposes of any Act of Parliament.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2006, c. 9, s. 259
:::

## [PART II]{.HLabel1}[Public Money]{.HTitleText1} {#h-228394 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public money

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Part, all public
  money shall be deposited to the credit of the Receiver General.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment of accounts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Receiver General may establish, in the name of
  the Receiver General, accounts for the deposit of public money with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any member of the Canadian Payments Association;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any local cooperative credit society that is a
    member of a central cooperative credit society having membership in
    the Canadian Payments Association;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any fiscal agent that the Minister may designate;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any financial institution outside Canada that the
    Minister may designate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record of public money

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who collects or receives public money
  shall keep a record of receipts and deposits thereof in such form and
  manner as the Treasury Board may prescribe by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of persons collecting public money

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any regulations made under subsection (5),
  every person employed in the collection or management of, or charged
  with the receipt of, public money and every other person who collects
  or receives public money shall pay that money to the credit of the
  Receiver General.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the manner in which public money shall
    be paid to the credit of the Receiver General;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorizing any person mentioned in any of
    paragraphs (2)(a) to (d) who has given credit to the Receiver
    General for an instruction for payment that is deposited in
    accordance with regulations made under paragraph (a) to charge the
    amount of the instruction back to the Receiver General in the manner
    specified by the regulations, where the instruction is dishonoured
    after having been credited to the Receiver General; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorizing persons who collect or receive public
    money to withhold their fees or commissions from payments of that
    money to the credit of the Receiver General.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 17
- 1991, c. 24, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[collection
agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[17.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[collection agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
  person, other than an employee of a department, who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} carries on the business of collecting debts for
    other persons; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is registered or licensed as a member of the legal
    profession or as a collector of debts in the province in which the
    person carries on the business of collecting debts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment to collection agency

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the direction of the Treasury Board, fees
  or commissions may be paid out of the Consolidated Revenue Fund to a
  collection agency for the collection of any amount that was owed to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of a province on account of
    taxes payable to that province and that, pursuant to an agreement,
    Canada is authorized to collect on behalf of the province.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 24, s. 5
:::

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1999, c. 26,
s. 20\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Charges for services or use of facilities

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Treasury Board,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by regulation prescribe the fees or charges to be
    paid for a service or the use of a facility provided by or on behalf
    of Her Majesty in right of Canada by the users or classes of users
    of the service or facility; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorize the appropriate Minister to prescribe by
    order those fees or charges, subject to such terms and conditions as
    may be specified by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount not to exceed cost

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Fees and charges for a service or the use of a
  facility provided by or on behalf of Her Majesty in right of Canada
  that are prescribed under subsection (1) or the amount of which is
  adjusted under section 19.2 may not exceed the cost to Her Majesty in
  right of Canada of providing the service or the use of the facility to
  the users or class of users.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By whom payable

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, [[users]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of Canada, other than a
    department; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of a province.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 19
- 1991, c. 24, s. 6
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Charges for rights and privileges

**[[19.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.1 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may, on the recommendation of the Treasury Board,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} by regulation prescribe the fees or charges to be
  paid for a right or privilege conferred by or on behalf of Her Majesty
  in right of Canada, by means of a licence, permit or other
  authorization, by the persons or classes of persons on whom the right
  or privilege is conferred; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorize the appropriate Minister to prescribe by
  order those fees or charges, subject to such terms and conditions as
  may be specified by the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 24, s. 6
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjustment of amounts

- **[[19.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  regulation or order under section 19 or 19.1 may prescribe rules for
  the adjustment, by such amounts or ratios as are referred to in the
  regulation or order, of the amount of the fee or charge, for such
  period as is specified in the regulation or order, but no such rules
  may provide for the consideration of any factors of adjustment that
  are not specified in the rules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of adjusted amount

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding that a regulation or order provides
  for the adjustment of the amount of a fee or charge for a period, its
  amount for the period is equal to its amount for the immediately
  preceding period unless the appropriate Minister, before the beginning
  of the period, publishes a notice in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) specifying the adjusted amount and
  the manner in which it was determined.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 24, s. 6
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations subject to other Acts

**[[19.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.3 .sectionLabel}** Regulations and
orders under sections 19 and 19.1 are subject to the provisions of any
Act of Parliament relating to the service or the use of the facility, or
to the right or privilege, but, for greater certainty, may be made even
though an Act of Parliament requires the provision of the service or
facility or the conferral of the right or privilege.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 24, s. 6
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of deposits

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  money is received by a public officer from any person as a deposit to
  ensure the doing of any act or thing, the public officer shall hold or
  dispose of the money in accordance with regulations of the Treasury
  Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of money

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where money is paid by any person to a public officer
  for any purpose that is not fulfilled, the money may, in accordance
  with regulations of the Treasury Board, be returned or repaid to that
  person, less such sum as in the opinion of the Board is properly
  attributable to any service rendered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of non-public money

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Money paid to the credit of the Receiver General that
  is not public money may be returned or repaid in accordance with
  regulations of the Treasury Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. F-10, s. 14
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Money received for special purpose

- **[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Money
  referred to in paragraph (d) of the definition [[public
  money]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in section 2 that is received by or on
  behalf of Her Majesty for a special purpose and paid into the
  Consolidated Revenue Fund may be paid out of the Consolidated Revenue
  Fund for that purpose, subject to any statute applicable thereto.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any other Act of Parliament, interest may
  be allowed and paid from the Consolidated Revenue Fund in respect of
  money to which subsection (1) applies, in accordance with and at rates
  fixed by the Minister with the approval of the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. F-10, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Money paid in respect of proceedings in
Parliament

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** Where the Senate or House
of Commons, by resolution or pursuant to any rule or standing order,
authorizes a refund of public money that was received in respect of any
proceedings before Parliament, the Receiver General may pay the refund
out of the Consolidated Revenue Fund.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. F-10, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section,

  [[other debt]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[other debt]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any amount owing to Her
      Majesty, other than a tax or penalty or an amount in respect of
      which subsection 24.1(2) applies; ([autre dette]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[penalty]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[penalty]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes any forfeiture or
      pecuniary penalty imposed or authorized to be imposed by any Act
      of Parliament for any contravention of the laws relating to the
      collection of the revenue, or to the management of any public work
      producing tolls or revenue, notwithstanding that part of such
      forfeiture or penalty is payable to the informer or prosecutor, or
      to any other person; ([pénalité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[tax]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[tax]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes any tax, impost, duty or toll
      payable to Her Majesty, imposed or authorized to be imposed by any
      Act of Parliament. ([taxes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remission of taxes and penalties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the appropriate Minister, remit any tax or penalty, including any
  interest paid or payable thereon, where the Governor in Council
  considers that the collection of the tax or the enforcement of the
  penalty is unreasonable or unjust or that it is otherwise in the
  public interest to remit the tax or penalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remission of other debts

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the Treasury Board, remit any other debt, including any interest
  paid or payable thereon, where the Governor in Council considers that
  the collection of the other debt is unreasonable or unjust or that it
  is otherwise in the public interest to remit the other debt.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remission may be partial, etc.

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A remission pursuant to this section may be total or
  partial or conditional or unconditional and may be granted

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before, after or pending any suit or proceeding for
    the recovery of the tax, penalty or other debt in respect of which
    the remission is granted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} before or after any payment of the tax, penalty or
    other debt has been made or enforced by process or execution; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a tax or other debt, in any
    particular case or class of cases and before the liability therefor
    arises.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form of remission

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A remission pursuant to this section may be granted

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by forbearing to institute a suit or proceeding for
    the recovery of the tax, penalty or other debt in respect of which
    the remission is granted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by delaying, staying or discontinuing any suit or
    proceeding already instituted;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} by forbearing to enforce, staying or abandoning any
    execution or process on any judgment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} by the entry of satisfaction on any judgment; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} by repaying any sum of money paid to or recovered
    by the Receiver General for the tax, penalty or other debt.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditional remission

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a remission is granted pursuant to this section
  subject to a condition and the condition is not fulfilled, the tax,
  penalty or other debt may be enforced, or all proceedings may be had,
  as if there had been no remission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of remission

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A conditional remission, on fulfilment of the
  condition, and an unconditional remission have effect as if the
  remission were made after the tax, penalty or other debt in respect of
  which it was granted had been sued for and recovered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Customs and excise

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} No tax paid to Her Majesty on any goods shall be
  remitted by reason only that, after the payment of the tax and after
  release from the control of customs or excise officers, the goods were
  lost or destroyed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of remission

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Where a penalty imposed by any law relating to the
  revenue has been wholly and unconditionally remitted pursuant to this
  section, the remission has the effect of a pardon for the offence for
  which the penalty was incurred, and thereafter the offence has no
  legal effect prejudicial to the person to whom the remission was
  granted.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 23
- 1991, c. 24, ss. 7, 50(F)
- 1999, c. 31, s. 102(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment out of C.R.F.

- **[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Remissions granted under this Act or
  any other Act of Parliament may be paid out of the Consolidated
  Revenue Fund.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report in Public Accounts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Remissions granted under this or any other Act of
  Parliament during a fiscal year shall be reported in the Public
  Accounts for that year in such form as the Treasury Board may direct.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 24
- 1991, c. 24, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forgiveness of debts and obligations

- **[[24.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no debt
  or obligation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that is included in the statement of assets and
    liabilities of Canada referred to in subparagraph 64(2)(a)(iii), the
    forgiveness of which would result in a charge to an appropriation,
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is owing by a Crown corporation to Her Majesty

  shall be forgiven in whole or in part otherwise than by or under an
  Act of Parliament, including an appropriation Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debts, etc., included in statement of
  assets and liabilities

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No debt or obligation referred to in paragraph (1)(a)
  shall be forgiven unless the amount to be forgiven is included as a
  budgetary expenditure in an appropriation Act or any other Act of
  Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forgiveness may be conditional

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a debt or obligation is forgiven pursuant to
  subsection (1),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the forgiveness may be conditional or
    unconditional;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the forgiveness is conditional and the condition
    is not fulfilled, the debt or obligation may be enforced, or all
    proceedings may be had, as if there had been no forgiveness; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a conditional forgiveness, on fulfilment of the
    condition, and an unconditional forgiveness release the person whose
    debt or obligation was forgiven from all further liability for the
    debt or obligation.
