#### [Implementation of Other Free Trade Agreements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229461 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directive

- **[[89.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections 85(1) to (1.2),
  the Governor in Council may give a directive under subsection 89(1) to
  any parent Crown corporation for the purpose of implementing any
  provision of a free trade agreement set out in Schedule VII that
  pertains to that Crown corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the Treasury Board and the appropriate Minister made at the request of
  a Crown corporation, make any regulations in relation to that
  corporation that the Governor in Council considers necessary for the
  purpose of implementing any provision of a free trade agreement set
  out in Schedule VII that pertains to that corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 18, s. 42
:::

#### [Orders --- Terms and Conditions of Employment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229469 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order --- unionized employees

- **[[89.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by
  order, direct a Crown corporation to have its negotiating mandate
  approved by the Treasury Board for the purpose of the Crown
  corporation entering into a collective agreement with a bargaining
  agent for a bargaining unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of Treasury Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Governor in Council directs a Crown
  corporation to have its negotiating mandate approved, the Treasury
  Board may impose any requirement on the Crown corporation with respect
  to that negotiating mandate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attendance and observation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Governor in Council directs a Crown
  corporation to have its negotiating mandate approved, the Treasury
  Board may require that an employee under the jurisdiction of the
  Secretary of the Treasury Board attend and observe the collective
  bargaining between the Crown corporation and the bargaining agent, in
  which case that employee is entitled to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collective agreement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A Crown corporation that is subject to an order made
  under subsection (1) may enter into a collective agreement referred to
  in the order only with the Treasury Board's approval.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 33, s. 229
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order --- non-unionized employees

- **[[89.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by
  order, direct a Crown corporation to obtain the Treasury Board's
  approval before the Crown corporation fixes the terms and conditions
  of employment of its non-unionized employees who are not appointed by
  the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of Treasury Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Governor in Council directs a Crown
  corporation to obtain the Treasury Board's approval, the Treasury
  Board may impose any requirement on the Crown corporation with respect
  to the terms and conditions of employment of those employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 33, s. 229
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directors' duty to comply

- **[[89.91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89.91
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The directors of a Crown corporation
  that is subject to an order made under subsection 89.8(1) or 89.9(1)
  shall ensure that subsection 89.8(4), the order and any requirement
  imposed under subsection 89.8(2) or 89.9(2) are complied with and, if
  in so doing they act in accordance with section 115, they are not
  accountable for any consequences arising from that compliance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Best interests

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Compliance by a Crown corporation with subsection
  89.8(4), the order or the requirement is deemed to be in the best
  interests of the corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 33, s. 229
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Treasury Board not employer

**[[89.92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89.92 .sectionLabel}** For greater
certainty, including for the purposes of the [Canada Labour
Code](/eng/acts/L-2), the Treasury Board is neither the employer of the
employees of the Crown corporation that is subject to an order made
under subsection 89.8(1) or 89.9(1) nor an employer representative of
the Crown corporation or a person acting on the Crown corporation's
behalf.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 33, s. 229
:::

#### [Restricted Transactions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229499 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transactions requiring Parliamentary
authorization

- **[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall, unless authorized by an Act of Parliament,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} procure the incorporation of a corporation any
    shares of which, on incorporation, would be held by, on behalf of or
    in trust for the Crown;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} acquire shares of a corporation that, on
    acquisition, would be held by, on behalf of or in trust for the
    Crown;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} apply for articles that would add to or otherwise
    make a material change in the objects or purposes for which a parent
    Crown corporation is incorporated, or the restrictions on the
    businesses or activities that a parent Crown corporation may carry
    on, as set out in its articles;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} sell or otherwise dispose of any shares of a parent
    Crown corporation; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} procure the dissolution or amalgamation of a parent
    Crown corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No parent Crown corporation shall, unless authorized
  by an Act of Parliament, sell or otherwise dispose of all or
  substantially all of the assets of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no corporation, or
  corporations, in a related group shall, unless authorized by an Act of
  Parliament, sell or otherwise dispose of any of the assets of the
  corporation or corporations, if the assets to be sold or otherwise
  disposed of constitute all or substantially all of the total assets of
  the group.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Holding by agent corporations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs (1)(a) and (b), shares
  held by a corporation that is an agent of the Crown

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are deemed not to be held by or on behalf of the
    Crown; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are not by reason of that fact alone shares held in
    trust for the Crown.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section and sections 91 to
  94,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reference to an acquisition or a sale or other
    disposal includes an acquisition or a sale or other disposal

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that is between agent corporations or between the
      Crown and an agent corporation, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} that occurs as a result of more than one related
      transaction or event;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a reference to the assets of a corporation or
    corporations includes shares of another corporation held by, on
    behalf of or in trust for the corporation or corporations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} [[person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes an agent of
    the Crown;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [[related group]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a group
    of corporations consisting of a parent Crown corporation and all of
    its wholly-owned subsidiaries; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} [[shares]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes any
    conversion or exchange privilege, option or right to acquire shares.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 90
- 1991, c. 24, s. 50(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transactions requiring Governor in
Council authorization

- **[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 90, no parent
  Crown corporation or wholly-owned subsidiary of a parent Crown
  corporation shall, unless authorized by the Governor in Council,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} procure the incorporation of a corporation any
    shares of which, on incorporation, would be held by, on behalf of or
    in trust for the corporation or subsidiary;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} acquire shares of a corporation that, on
    acquisition, would be held by, on behalf of or in trust for the
    corporation or subsidiary;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} acquire all or substantially all of the assets of
    another corporation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} sell or otherwise dispose of any shares of a
    wholly-owned subsidiary of the corporation; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} procure the dissolution or amalgamation of a
    wholly-owned subsidiary of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 90 and subsection (3), no
  corporation, or corporations, in a related group shall, unless
  authorized by the Governor in Council, sell or otherwise dispose of
  any of the assets of the corporation or corporations used in any major
  business or activity of the corporation or corporations, if the assets
  to be sold or otherwise disposed of constitute all or substantially
  all of the total assets of the group used in that major business or
  activity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 90, no wholly-owned subsidiary
  shall, unless authorized by the Governor in Council,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sell or otherwise dispose of any shares of the
    subsidiary; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sell or otherwise dispose of all or substantially
    all of the assets of the subsidiary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 90, no person shall, unless
  authorized by the Governor in Council, apply for articles in respect
  of a parent Crown corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inclusion in corporate plan

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a parent Crown corporation sets out the details
  of a proposed transaction in a corporate plan submitted pursuant to
  section 122, the Governor in Council, in addition to approving the
  plan under that section, may authorize a person to undertake the
  transaction for the purposes of this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to execute transaction required

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No person shall be authorized under this section to
  undertake a transaction unless the Governor in Council is satisfied
  that the person is otherwise empowered to undertake the transaction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms and conditions

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, in any authorization
  under this section, specify such terms and conditions as the Governor
  in Council deems appropriate and may make the authorization either one
  of general application or in respect of any specified transaction or
  transactions.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 91
- 1991, c. 24, ss. 25, 50(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

- **[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Sections 90 and 91 do not apply in
  respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the acquisition of shares or assets by way of
    security only, or the sale or other disposal of shares or assets
    held by way of security only;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the acquisition or the sale or other disposal of
    shares or assets in the ordinary course of a business of providing
    financial assistance;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the acquisition or the sale or other disposal of
    shares or assets, if the acquisition, sale or disposal involves only

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a parent Crown corporation and one or more
      wholly-owned subsidiaries of the corporation, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} two or more wholly-owned subsidiaries

    and is made as part of a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"}
    reorganization of the parent Crown corporation or wholly-owned
    subsidiaries;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the acquisition of shares by way of the exercise of
    any conversion or exchange privilege, option or right to acquire
    shares, if that privilege, option or right was acquired in
    accordance with section 90 or 91, as the case may be;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the acquisition of shares of a parent Crown
    corporation by the corporation; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the acquisition of shares of a wholly-owned
    subsidiary by the subsidiary, the parent Crown corporation that
    wholly owns the subsidiary or any other wholly-owned subsidiary of
    that parent Crown corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs 90(1)(b) and 91(1)(b) do not apply in
  respect of the nomination or appointment of a person to the board of
  directors or membership of a corporation, except where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a majority of the directors or members are
    nominated or appointed by or on behalf of the Crown or by directors
    or members who were nominated or appointed by or on behalf of the
    Crown; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Crown would otherwise obtain control of the
    corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
  exempting a specified parent Crown corporation or wholly-owned
  subsidiary or a parent Crown corporation or wholly-owned subsidiary of
  a specified class from the application of section 91, either generally
  or in respect of any transaction of a specified class.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 92
- 1991, c. 24, s. 26
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

- **[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person undertakes a transaction referred to in section 90 or 91,
  that person shall notify the prescribed person in accordance with the
  regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the Governor in
  Council may make regulations prescribing the person to be notified and
  the time within which and manner in which that person is to be
  notified.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissolution

- **[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 90, where a
  parent Crown corporation is not named in Schedule III within a period
  of sixty days after its incorporation or acquisition, the directors
  and shareholders of the corporation shall forthwith after the
  expiration of that period take all steps necessary to dissolve the
  corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissolution or sale

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 91, the Governor in Council
  may direct that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any wholly-owned subsidiary incorporated contrary
    to section 91 be dissolved or otherwise disposed of,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any shares of a corporation acquired contrary to
    section 91 be sold or otherwise disposed of,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any change effected by articles contrary to section
    91 be reversed, or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any assets acquired contrary to section 91 be sold
    or otherwise disposed of,

  and the directors and shareholders of the corporation in respect of
  which the direction is given shall forthwith take all steps necessary
  to comply with the direction.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 94
- 1991, c. 24, s. 27
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No inconsistent business or activity

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  parent Crown corporation or wholly-owned subsidiary of a parent Crown
  corporation shall carry on any business or activity that is not
  consistent with the objects or purposes for which the parent Crown
  corporation is incorporated, or the restrictions on the businesses or
  activities that it may carry on, as set out in its charter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Savings

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) shall not be construed as prohibiting
  or restricting a parent Crown corporation or wholly-owned subsidiary
  from continuing to carry on any business or activity that it carried
  on immediately before September 1, 1984.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

#### [Crown Agency Status]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229597 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercise of powers

**[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** An agent corporation may
exercise its powers only as an agent of the Crown.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Name in contracts

**[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** An agent corporation may
enter into contracts in the name of the Crown or in the name of the
corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Legal proceedings

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** Actions, suits or other
legal proceedings in respect of any right or obligation acquired or
incurred by an agent corporation, whether in the name of the Crown or in
the name of the corporation, may be brought or taken by or against the
corporation in the name of the corporation in any court that would have
jurisdiction if the corporation were not an agent of the Crown.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Property of the Crown

- **[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  the title to property is held in the name of an agent corporation,
  that property is the property of the Crown.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal of property

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section and sections 90, 91 and 130,
  an agent corporation may sell or otherwise dispose of or lease any
  property held by the corporation and may retain and use the proceeds
  of the disposal or lease, but only

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the regulations; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on the authorization of the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply in respect of any sale
  or other disposal or lease of property by an agent corporation
  established by an Act of Parliament, if the corporation is
  specifically empowered by that Act or any other Act of Parliament

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to sell or otherwise dispose of or lease property;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to sell or otherwise dispose of or lease property
    for consideration not exceeding a specified amount and the sale or
    other disposal or lease of the property is for consideration equal
    to or less than the specified amount.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2), the Governor in
  Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the terms and conditions on which an
    agent corporation may sell or otherwise dispose of or lease
    property;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the circumstances in which an agent
    corporation may retain and use all or any part of the proceeds of
    any disposal or lease of property; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} exempting a specified agent corporation or an agent
    corporation of a specified class from the application of paragraphs
    (2)(a) and (b), either generally or in respect of any specified
    property or any property of a specified class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms and conditions

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, in any authorizing order
  under subsection (2), specify any terms and conditions that the
  Governor in Council considers appropriate, including terms and
  conditions respecting the retention and use of all or any part of the
  proceeds of the disposal or lease by the agent corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision and Acts not applicable

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Section 61 of this Act, the [Surplus Crown Assets
  Act](/eng/acts/S-27), and the [Federal Real Property and Federal
  Immovables Act](/eng/acts/F-8.4), except paragraphs 16(1)(g) and (h)
  and (2)(g) and subsection 18(6), do not apply to an agent corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 99
- 1991, c. 24, s. 28, c. 50, s. 28
- 2001, c. 4, s. 161
- 2009, c. 2, s. 370
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-99-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 99"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security interests

- **[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no agent
  corporation, for the purposes of securing payment of a debt or
  performance of an obligation, shall charge, mortgage, hypothecate,
  cede and transfer, pledge or otherwise create an interest in or charge
  on any property held by the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any terms and conditions set out in the
  designation, an agent corporation designated by the Minister may
  pledge any securities or cash that it holds, or give deposits, as
  security for the payment or performance of any obligation of the
  corporation arising out of any derivative that it enters into or
  guarantees for the management of financial risks.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 100
- 2013, c. 40, s. 270
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-100-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 100"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Borrowing

- **[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  agent corporation shall borrow money otherwise than from the Crown,
  unless the corporation is both

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} empowered by an Act of Parliament to so borrow
    money; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specifically authorized by any Act of Parliament,
    including an appropriation Act, to so borrow money.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leases

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite any regulations made under paragraph
  127(4)(b), subsection (1) does not apply in respect of leases, as
  defined in the Handbook of the Chartered Professional Accountants of
  Canada, as amended from time to time.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 101
- 2018, c. 12, s. 200
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-101-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 101"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Third Parties]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229648 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assertions

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Crown corporation may not assert against a person dealing with the
  corporation or with any person who has acquired rights from the
  corporation that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} this Part or the regulations, the charter or
    by-laws of the corporation or any directive given to the corporation
    have not been complied with,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person held out by the corporation as a director,
    officer or agent of the corporation has not been duly appointed or
    has no authority to exercise the powers and perform the duties that
    are customary in the business or activity of the corporation or
    usual for such a director, officer or agent, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a document issued by any director, officer or agent
    of the corporation having apparent authority to issue the document
    is not valid or genuine by reason only that the director, officer or
    agent lacked actual authority to issue the document,

  except where the person has knowledge that the facts asserted are
  true.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A certificate stating that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any particular transaction has been authorized by
    the Governor in Council pursuant to section 91 or 99, has or has not
    been directed by the Governor in Council pursuant to section 94 or
    has been approved by the Minister of Finance pursuant to subsection
    127(3),

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any particular transaction is consistent with a
    corporate plan or any amendment thereto approved pursuant to section
    122, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any particular capital expenditure or commitment to
    make a capital expenditure is included in a capital budget, an item
    in a capital budget or an amendment to a capital budget that has
    been approved pursuant to section 124

  and that is signed by the chairperson or chief executive officer of a
  Crown corporation is sufficient confirmation of the statement to any
  person, except where the person has knowledge to the contrary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence of authorization, direction or
  approval

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The absence of any authorization, direction or
  approval referred to in subsection (2) does not affect any right or
  remedy otherwise available to any person, except where the person
  knows of such absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Invalidity

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No action of a Crown corporation, including a
  transfer of property, is invalid by reason only that the corporation
  was without the capacity or power to so act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No constructive notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No person dealing with a Crown corporation or with
  any person who has acquired rights from a Crown corporation shall be
  deemed to have notice or knowledge of the contents of a document,
  other than an Act of Parliament or any instrument required to be
  published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) pursuant
  to the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22), concerning the
  corporation by reason only that the document has been made public.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 102
- 2005, c. 30, s. 133(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-102-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 102"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229670 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision not applicable

**[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** Section 268 of the
[Canada Business Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-44) does not apply to a
parent Crown corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 103
- 1994, c. 24, s. 34(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Act not applicable

**[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** The [Canada
Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-1.8), chapter C-32 of the Revised Statutes
of Canada, 1970, does not apply to a parent Crown corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

### [DIVISION II]{.HLabel2}[Directors and Officers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-229679 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229680 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[officer-director]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[104.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,
[[officer-director]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a parent Crown
corporation, means the chairperson and the chief executive officer of
the corporation, by whatever name called.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 24, s. 29
- 2004, c. 16, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-104.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 104.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Appointment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229685 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of directors

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each
  director, other than an officer-director, of a parent Crown
  corporation shall be appointed by the appropriate Minister, with the
  approval of the Governor in Council, to hold office during pleasure
  for a term not exceeding four years that will ensure, as far as
  possible, the expiration in any one year of the terms of office of not
  more than one half of the directors of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officer or employee not to be director

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No officer or employee of a Crown corporation or any
  of its affiliates, other than the chief executive officer of a parent
  Crown corporation, shall be a director of the parent Crown
  corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transitional provision

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Any officer or employee of a Crown corporation or
  any of its affiliates, other than the chief executive officer of a
  parent Crown corporation, who was a director of the parent Crown
  corporation immediately before the day on which this subsection comes
  into force may continue as a director of that corporation for six
  months after that day or, if it is shorter, the remainder of his or
  her term.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-appointment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A director of a parent Crown corporation is eligible
  for re-appointment on the expiration of his term of office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation in office

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), if a director of a parent
  Crown corporation is not appointed to take office on the expiration of
  the term of an incumbent director, other than an officer-director, the
  incumbent director continues in office until his or her successor is
  appointed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of officer-directors

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Each officer-director of a parent Crown corporation
  shall be appointed by the Governor in Council to hold office during
  pleasure for such term as the Governor in Council considers
  appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Before an officer-director of a parent Crown
  corporation is appointed, the appropriate Minister shall consult the
  board of directors of the corporation with respect to the appointment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of subordinate officers

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any other Act of Parliament that was in
  force on September 1, 1984, the board of directors of a parent Crown
  corporation is responsible for the appointment of officers of the
  corporation, other than officer-directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifications preserved

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section is to be construed as
  empowering the appointment or re-appointment as a director or
  officer-director of a parent Crown corporation, or the continuation in
  office as a director of a parent Crown corporation, of any person who
  does not meet any qualifications for the appointment, re-appointment
  or continuation established by any other Act of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply to an [ex
  officio]{.otherLang lang="la"} director or officer-director of a
  parent Crown corporation.

- [(10)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1991, c. 24, s. 30\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 105
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (4th Supp.), s. 44(E)
- 1991, c. 24, s. 30
- 2004, c. 16, s. 8
- 2006, c. 9, s. 267
- 2009, c. 2, s. 371
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-105-20061212.html "Link to previous version of section 105"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of acts

**[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** An act of a director,
chairperson, chief executive officer or other officer of a parent Crown
corporation is not invalid by reason only of an irregularity in his
appointment or a defect in his qualifications.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 106
- 2005, c. 30, s. 133(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-106-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 106"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Resignation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229714 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resignation

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  resignation of a director, chairperson or chief executive officer of a
  parent Crown corporation becomes effective at the time the corporation
  receives a written resignation from him or at the time specified in
  the resignation, whichever is later.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of resignation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A parent Crown corporation shall send a copy of the
  resignation to the Clerk of the Privy Council within fifteen days
  after the parent Crown corporation receives it.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 107
- 1991, c. 24, s. 31
- 2005, c. 30, s. 133(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-107-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 107"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Remuneration]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229722 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  rate of any remuneration paid to a director, chairperson or chief
  executive officer of a parent Crown corporation for his services in
  respect of that office and, in the case of a chairperson or chief
  executive officer, any other office of the corporation or an affiliate
  thereof shall be fixed by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other benefits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any benefits, other than remuneration, provided to a
  director, chairperson or chief executive officer of a parent Crown
  corporation for his services in respect of that office and, in the
  case of a chairperson or chief executive officer, any other office of
  the corporation or an affiliate thereof shall be fixed by the board of
  directors of the corporation in accordance with the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Wholly-owned subsidiary

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The rate of any remuneration paid to a director,
  chairperson or chief executive officer of a wholly-owned subsidiary
  who is not also an officer-director of the parent Crown corporation
  for the director's, chairperson's or officer's services in respect of
  that subsidiary shall be fixed by the board of directors of the parent
  Crown corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, the Governor in
  Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} defining the term
    [[remuneration]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the provision and fixing of benefits,
    other than remuneration, to or for any director, chairperson or
    chief executive officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 108
- 1991, c. 24, s. 32
- 2005, c. 30, s. 133(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-108-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 108"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Management]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229736 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Management

**[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** Subject to this Part,
the board of directors of a Crown corporation is responsible for the
management of the businesses, activities and other affairs of the
corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resolution in lieu of meeting

- **[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  resolution in writing, signed by all the directors of a Crown
  corporation entitled to vote on that resolution at a meeting of the
  board of directors or a committee of directors of the corporation is
  as valid as if it had been passed at a meeting of the board of
  directors or a committee of directors, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing resolution

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of every resolution referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be kept with the minutes of the proceedings of
  the board of directors or committee of directors, as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissent

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director of a Crown corporation who is present at a meeting of the
  board of directors or a committee of directors of the corporation is
  deemed to have consented to any resolution passed or action taken at
  that meeting unless the director

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} requests that written notice of his dissent be or
    written notice of his dissent is entered in the minutes of the
    meeting;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} gives written notice of his dissent to the
    secretary of the meeting before the meeting is adjourned; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} sends written notice of his dissent by registered
    mail or delivers it to the head office of the corporation
    immediately after the meeting is adjourned.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loss of right of dissent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director of a Crown corporation who votes for or
  consents to a resolution is not entitled to dissent in respect of that
  resolution pursuant to subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissent of absent director

**[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** A director of a Crown
corporation who was not present at a meeting of the board of directors
or a committee of directors of the corporation at which a resolution was
passed or action taken is deemed to have consented thereto unless within
seven days after becoming aware of the resolution or action the director

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} causes written notice of his dissent to be placed
  with the minutes of the meeting; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} sends written notice of his dissent by registered
  mail or delivers it to the head office of the corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Participation by telephone, etc.

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** A director of a Crown
corporation may, subject to the by-laws of the corporation, participate
in a meeting of the board of directors or a committee of directors of
the corporation by means of such telephone or other communications
facilities as permit all persons participating in the meeting to hear
each other, and a director participating in such a meeting by such means
is deemed for the purposes of this Part to be present at the meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public meeting

- **[[113.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The board of directors of a parent
  Crown corporation shall hold a public meeting within 18 months after
  the day on which the board holds its first meeting or, if it is later,
  the day on which this section comes into force and, subsequently,
  within 15 months after the day on which the last preceding public
  meeting was held.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-laws

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The meeting shall be held in Canada in the manner
  provided for in the by-laws or, if no manner is provided for, in the
  manner determined by the board of directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of meeting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The corporation shall publish a notice of the meeting
  at least 30 days before the day on which the meeting is to be held.
  The notice shall indicate the location, if any, and the date and time
  of the meeting, the means of participating in the meeting and how
  copies of the corporation's most recent annual report may be obtained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Director and chief executive officer to
  attend

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} One or more directors of the corporation and its
  chief executive officer, whether or not he or she is a director of the
  corporation, shall participate in the meeting to answer questions from
  the public.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2009, c. 2, s. 372
:::

#### [By-laws]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229779 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-laws

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  board of directors of a parent Crown corporation may, by resolution,
  make, amend or repeal any by-laws that regulate the business or
  affairs of the corporation, unless the charter or by-laws of the
  corporation otherwise provide.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of by-law to appropriate Minister
  and President of the Treasury Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The board of directors of a parent Crown corporation
  shall, after making, amending or repealing a by-law, send a copy of
  that by-law to the appropriate Minister and the President of the
  Treasury Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment or repeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may direct the board of
  directors of a parent Crown corporation to make, amend or repeal a
  by-law within such period as is specified in the direction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the form or the content of by-laws of
    parent Crown corporations and the time at, before or within which
    copies of by-laws are to be sent to the appropriate Minister and the
    President of the Treasury Board; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exempting a specified parent Crown corporation or a
    parent Crown corporation of a specified class from the application
    of subsection (2), either generally or in respect of any specified
    by-law or any by-law of a specified class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No direction shall be given pursuant to
  subsection (3) to the Standards Council of Canada with respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which voluntary standardization is
    promoted; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the provision of financial assistance to or for the
    benefit of a particular person or group of persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision not applicable

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Section 103 of the [Canada Business Corporations
  Act](/eng/acts/C-44) does not apply in respect of any parent Crown
  corporation established under that Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 114
- 1991, c. 24, s. 33
- 1994, c. 24, s. 34(F)
:::

#### [Duties]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229799 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of care of directors and officers

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every director and every officer of
  a Crown corporation in exercising his powers and performing his duties
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} act honestly and in good faith with a view to the
    best interests of the corporation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exercise the care, diligence and skill that a
    reasonably prudent person would exercise in comparable
    circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to comply

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every director and every officer of a parent Crown
  corporation or of a wholly-owned subsidiary of a parent Crown
  corporation shall comply with this Part, the regulations, the charter
  and by-laws of the corporation or subsidiary and any directive given
  to the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reliance on statements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A director or officer is not liable for a breach of
  his duty under subsection (1) or (2) if he relies in good faith on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} financial statements of the Crown corporation
    represented to him by an officer of the corporation or in a written
    report of the auditor of the corporation as fairly reflecting the
    financial condition of the corporation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a report of a lawyer, accountant, engineer,
    appraiser or other person whose position or profession lends
    credibility to a statement made by him.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of interest in contract

- **[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director or officer of a Crown corporation who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is a party to a material contract or proposed
    material contract with the corporation, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is a director or officer of, or has a material
    interest in any person who is a party to, a material contract or
    proposed material contract with the corporation,

  shall disclose in writing to the corporation or request to have
  entered in the minutes of a meeting of the board of directors thereof
  the nature and extent of his interest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for disclosure by director

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The disclosure required by subsection (1) shall be
  made, in the case of a director,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the meeting of the board of directors at which
    the proposed contract is first considered;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the director was not then interested in the
    proposed contract, at the first meeting of the board of directors
    after he becomes so interested;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the director becomes interested after the
    contract is made, at the first meeting of the board of directors
    after he becomes so interested; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the director was interested in the contract
    before becoming a director, at the first meeting of the board of
    directors after he becomes a director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for disclosure by officer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The disclosure required by subsection (1) shall be
  made, in the case of an officer who is not a director,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} forthwith after he becomes aware that the contract
    or proposed contract is to be considered or has been considered at a
    meeting of the board of directors;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the officer becomes interested after the
    contract is made, forthwith after he becomes so interested; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the officer was interested in the contract
    before becoming an officer, forthwith after he becomes an officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for disclosure by director or
  officer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a material contract or proposed material contract
  is one that, in the ordinary course of the business or activity of the
  Crown corporation, would not require approval by the board of
  directors, a director or officer shall disclose in writing to the
  corporation or request to have entered in the minutes of a meeting of
  the board of directors the nature and extent of his interest forthwith
  after the director or officer becomes aware of the contract or
  proposed contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voting

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A director referred to in subsection (1) shall not
  vote on any resolution to approve the contract unless the contract is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an arrangement by way of security for money lent to
    or obligations undertaken by him for the benefit of the Crown
    corporation or a subsidiary of the Crown corporation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one relating primarily to his remuneration or
    benefits as a director, officer, employee or agent of the Crown
    corporation or a subsidiary of the Crown corporation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} one for indemnity or insurance under section 119;
    or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} one with an affiliate of the Crown corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing disclosure

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, a general notice to
  the board of directors by a director or officer, declaring that he is
  a director or officer of or has a material interest in a person and is
  to be regarded as interested in any contract made with that person, is
  a sufficient declaration of interest in relation to any contract so
  made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[board of
  directors]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In this section and section 117, [[board of
  directors]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a committee of directors.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Avoidance standards

**[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** A material contract
between a Crown corporation and one or more of its directors or
officers, or between a Crown corporation and another person of which a
director or an officer of the Crown corporation is a director or an
officer or in which he has a material interest, is neither void nor
voidable by reason only of that relationship or by reason only that a
director with an interest in the contract is present at or is counted to
determine the presence of a quorum at a meeting of the board of
directors that authorized the contract, if the director or officer
disclosed his interest in accordance with subsection 116(2), (3), (4) or
(6), as the case may be, and the contract was approved by the board of
directors and it was reasonable and fair to the Crown corporation at the
time it was approved.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a director or an officer of a
  Crown corporation fails to disclose his interest in a material
  contract in accordance with section 116, a court may, on application
  of the corporation or on behalf of the Crown, set aside the contract
  on such terms as it thinks fit.

- Definition of [[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the Province of Newfoundland and Labrador, the
    Trial Division of the Supreme Court of the Province;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} in the Province of Ontario, the Superior Court of
    Justice;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the Province of Quebec, the Superior Court of
    the Province;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the Province of New Brunswick, Manitoba,
    Saskatchewan or Alberta, the Court of Queen's Bench for the
    Province;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the Provinces of Nova Scotia, British Columbia
    and Prince Edward Island, the Supreme Court of the Province; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in Yukon or the Northwest Territories, the Supreme
    Court, and in Nunavut, the Nunavut Court of Justice.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 118
- R.S., 1985, c. 27 (2nd Supp.), s. 10
- 1990, c. 17, s. 19
- 1992, c. 51, s. 49
- 1998, c. 30, ss. 13(F), 15(E)
- 1999, c. 3, s. 63
- 2002, c. 7, s. 172(E)
- 2015, c. 3, s. 95
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-118-20030401.html "Link to previous version of section 118"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Indemnification]{.HTitleText3} {#h-229861 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to indemnify

- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Treasury Board shall, in accordance with the regulations, if any,
  indemnify a present or former director or officer of a Crown
  corporation or a person who acts or acted at the request of a Minister
  or a Crown corporation as a director or officer of another
  corporation, and his or her heirs and legal representatives, against
  the costs, charges and expenses, including an amount paid to settle an
  action or satisfy a judgment, that are reasonably incurred by him or
  her in respect of any civil, criminal, administrative or investigative
  action or proceeding to which he or she is a party by reason of being
  or having been such a director or officer, if he or she

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} acted honestly and in good faith with a view to the
    best interests of the Crown corporation or other corporation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any criminal or administrative
    action or proceeding that is enforced by a monetary penalty,
    believed on reasonable grounds that the director's or officer's
    conduct was lawful.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advance of costs

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board shall, in accordance with the
  regulations, if any, advance moneys to any individual referred to in
  subsection (1) for the costs, charges and expenses of an action or
  proceeding referred to in that subsection unless there are reasonable
  grounds to believe that the individual does not fulfil the conditions
  set out in paragraph (1)(a) or (b). The individual shall repay the
  moneys if he or she does not fulfil those conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement to indemnification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite anything in this section, a director or
  officer referred to in subsection (1) is, and his or her heirs and
  legal representatives are, entitled to indemnity, in accordance with
  the regulations, if any, from the Treasury Board in respect of the
  costs, charges and expenses reasonably incurred by him or her in
  connection with the defence of any civil, criminal, administrative or
  investigative action or proceeding to which he or she was made a party
  by reason of being or having been such a director or officer, if he or
  she

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} was substantially successful on the merits of the
    defence of the action or proceeding; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fulfils the conditions set out in paragraphs (1)(a)
    and (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting indemnification and advances under this
    section, including terms and conditions governing them;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} defining, for the purposes of this section, the
    meaning of any word or expression used in it that is not defined in
    this Act;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prescribing circumstances in which a director or
    officer is presumed not to have fulfilled the condition set out in
    paragraph (1)(a); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the determination of the amounts to be
    paid as indemnification or advances under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payable out of C.R.F.

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any amount payable in respect of indemnification
  under this section may be paid out of the Consolidated Revenue Fund.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. F-11, s. 119
- 1991, c. 24, s. 34
- 1999, c. 31, s. 115(F)
- 2009, c. 2, s. 373
:::
